# RunPod Lift Architecture — research findings

## Up front: where my framing held up and where it didn't

The "lift the generic core down into `runpod-lifecycle`, make vibecomfy and Astrid peer consumers" plan is sound. Two important corrections though:

1. **`runpod-lifecycle` is already further along than the framing implied.** `discovery.get_pod(pod_id, config)` is a stateless reattach-by-id that returns a fully usable `Pod`. `terminate(pod_id, api_key)` works without a `Pod` handle at all. `EventHooks(on_state_change, on_error)` exists for streaming. So the cross-process pod-handle problem is mostly solved — what's missing is upload/exec/download/manifest, not reattach.
2. **Astrid has no "composite executor that wraps try/finally" idiom.** Executors are subprocesses that return; orchestrators are plans of steps (`code`/`attested`/`nested`) with `repeat.until` wrappers; there is no `on_failure`/`finally` step kind in `astrid/core/task/plan.py` or in the DSL (`astrid/orchestrate/dsl.py`). This is the single biggest design constraint on the lift — guaranteed teardown has to be solved either inside a single "one-shot" composite executor, or by a sweeper, not by orchestrator semantics that don't exist.

---

## Part A — what's generic vs vibecomfy-specific in `runpod_runner.py`

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/scripts/runpod_runner.py` (1178 lines). Classification:

**GENERIC — belongs in `runpod-lifecycle`:**
- `UploadHeartbeat` — pure progress logger.
- `install_signal_handlers` / `_clear_current_task_cancellation` — generic loop SIGINT/SIGTERM glue. Worth keeping as opt-in (Astrid orchestrators won't want their parent's signal handlers stomped — see watchdog discussion below).
- `should_skip`, `_iter_upload_files`, `_estimate_upload_payload`, `_preflight_upload_disk`, `_build_upload_tarball`, `_upload_tarball` — the entire upload mechanism (sftp_walk + tarball modes). The only vibecomfy-specific bit is the `/tmp/vibecomfy-upload.tar.gz` filename, easily parameterised.
- `upload_dir` — generic SFTP-walk uploader. The `should_skip(child, ROOT, exclude_set)` call hard-codes `ROOT`; fix by passing `root` explicitly.
- `_upload_remote_script` — generic; remote path `/tmp/vibecomfy-remote-run.sh` is the only brand.
- `_download_artifacts` — *core download flow* (archive a remote dir, sftp.get, tar extract) is generic. The actual paths it cares about (`out/corpus_matrix output out/runs`) are vibecomfy-specific defaults.
- `_sha256`, `_file_record`, `_display_path`, `_load_json`, `_md` — generic utilities.
- `run_pod` and `run_pod_detached` — the *shape* is generic (launch → wait_ready → gpu_check → upload → exec → poll → download → terminate). The watchdog wrapping (`PodGuard`) is generic. The polling loop *body* is mostly generic; what's vibecomfy-specific is the embedded `status_command` heredoc.

**VIBECOMFY-SPECIFIC — stays in vibecomfy:**
- `_runpod_config_kwargs` — entirely vibecomfy env var conventions (`VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_STORAGE`, `VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_GPU`, the disk-size overrides). This is just *how vibecomfy populates a `RunPodConfig`*; the lifecycle config already accepts `**overrides`, so this stays caller-side.
- `REMOTE_ROOT = "/workspace/vibecomfy"` — vibecomfy-specific default, but the value itself is just a path. Parameterise to caller.
- `DEFAULT_UPLOAD_EXCLUDES` — has vibecomfy-specific entries (`workflow_corpus`, `custom_nodes`, `vendor`). The *concept* of an exclude set is generic; the *contents* aren't.
- The detached `launch_command` shell snippet — references `out/corpus_matrix/exit_code` as the sentinel file. The pattern "write rc to a sentinel file" is generic; the path is vibecomfy.
- The `status_command` heredoc inside the poll loop — entirely vibecomfy. `results.tsv`, `ready_results.tsv`, `out/corpus_matrix`, `--- MEDIA ---` find filter, `vibecomfy-remote-live.log`. This is genuinely report-format-specific.
- `_parse_tsv`, `_image_info`/`_png_info`, `_collect_outputs`, `_collect_run_metadata`, `_collect_watchdogs`, `_collect_remote_logs`, `_build_artifact_manifest`, `_write_artifact_report`, `_print_detached_summary`, `_finalize_artifacts`, `_count_failures`, `_extract_prompt_id` — *all* of this is vibecomfy artifact-format code. It reads vibecomfy's `out/runs/*/metadata.json`, vibecomfy's `out/runs/*/watchdog.json`, vibecomfy's `results.tsv` schema, vibecomfy's `output/` images. The `manifest.json`/`report.md` shape is invented for this script; it's not Astrid-aligned (see Part C).

**MIXED — needs a careful split:**
- **`PodGuard`** — the launch/terminate wrapper is generic. The in-process watchdog (`_terminate_after`) is generic *but* relies on the calling process staying alive — which is exactly what breaks in the Astrid-executor case. Lift `PodGuard` but rename the watchdog "in-process max-runtime", and stamp a `terminate_at` field into a handle file the lifecycle returns. The handle-based watchdog enforcement is a Part D follow-up.
- **`run_pod` / `run_pod_detached`** — the orchestration shape is generic, but in their current form they hard-code `ROOT`, `REMOTE_ROOT`, the vibecomfy launch_command, the vibecomfy status_command, and the vibecomfy artifact-collection path. Lift them as `ship_and_run(...)` / `ship_and_run_detached(...)` taking explicit `local_root`, `remote_root`, `remote_script`, `excludes`, `progress_pattern`, and `artifact_paths`. Vibecomfy keeps a thin wrapper that injects its defaults.
- **`_parse_detached_exit`** — looks generic (parses `--- EXIT ---` sentinel from stdout) but it *only* works because vibecomfy's own poll-loop script writes that sentinel. Pair with a generic "poll-loop emits structured status lines" convention. The lifecycle should ship a default poll script that emits `=== POLL ts ===` / `--- EXIT ---`; vibecomfy can override and still parse with the same helper.

The cleanest split is: lift everything *up to and including* `_download_artifacts` (i.e. extract a `.tar.gz` to a local dir, return its path); leave *everything that reads and summarises the extracted contents* in vibecomfy.

---

## Part B — what `runpod-lifecycle` needs to grow

Reading `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/*`:

1. **Stateless reattach by id: already there.** `discovery.get_pod(pod_id, config)` returns a working `Pod` that lazily fetches SSH details on first use (`_ensure_ssh_details`). No coroutine state required — perfect for the Astrid cross-executor case. The only thing it doesn't preserve is `ram_tier` / `storage_volume`, but those are only used by `check_storage_health`; doesn't matter for exec/upload/download.

2. **Events API: exists but is the wrong primitive for upload heartbeats / poll-loop output.** `EventHooks` is for *state transitions* (PROVISIONING/STARTING/READY/...). The upload progress lines and detached poll snapshots are essentially log streams. They should be plain stdout (the current behavior) or a separate callback parameter. Don't overload `PodEvent`.

3. **`RunPodConfig.from_env`: hard-coded `RUNPOD_*` prefix.** It doesn't accept a consumer prefix. Vibecomfy currently overrides via `from_env(**kwargs)` rather than via env vars (in `_runpod_config_kwargs`), which is the right pattern. The package shouldn't grow `VIBECOMFY_*` / `ASTRID_*` env var support — that's caller-side. The `RUNPOD_*` env vars stay the lifecycle source of truth.

4. **CLI scope.** Today `cli.py` has `list / status / terminate / find-orphans / gpu-types`. After the lift, the right additions are:
   - `runpod-lifecycle launch [--name ...] [--detach]` — launch + print pod_id + handle path; exit. Detached because Astrid `provision` is a one-shot.
   - `runpod-lifecycle exec <pod_id> -- <cmd>` — attach + exec; useful debug and orchestrator step.
   - `runpod-lifecycle ship <pod_id> --local <dir> --remote <path>` — upload only.
   - `runpod-lifecycle fetch <pod_id> --remote <path> --local <dir>` — download artifacts.
   - `runpod-lifecycle run <pod_id> --script <file>` — the one-shot exec wrapper.
   
   I would *not* lift the all-in-one `ship_and_run` to the CLI surface initially — orchestrators compose primitives, and a one-shot CLI verb invites people to skip the orchestrator. Library only at first.

5. **`Pod` needs one new method: `upload_path(local, remote, *, mode='auto', excludes=(), on_progress=None)`** (covering sftp_walk + tarball) and **`download_archive(remote_paths, local_dir)`**. Currently `pod.open_ssh_client()` is the escape hatch and works fine — but raising these to first-class API lets the runner code live in one place and lets Astrid get them for free.

---

## Part C — what the Astrid pack needs to look like

From reading `astrid/core/executor/runner.py`, `astrid/core/orchestrator/runner.py`, `astrid/orchestrate/dsl.py`, `astrid/core/task/plan.py`, the three existing external packs, and `docs/creating-tools.md`:

1. **Inter-step state = artifact files only.** There is no shared in-memory context across executor invocations. Executors are spawned as subprocesses (`subprocess.run` in `_run_external_executor`), get their inputs as `--input key=value` and outputs through `{out}` / `path_template` placeholders, and produce files in `out/`. A **`pod_handle.json`** artifact written by `provision` and consumed by `exec`/`teardown` is the only honest pattern.

2. **Gate/verify schema is `Check`-based, not a fixed manifest shape.** `astrid/verify/checks.py` registers `file_nonempty`, `json_file`, `json_schema`, etc.; each `produces` declares a `(path, check)` pair. The runner's invented `manifest.json` schema is *not* what Astrid expects — Astrid wants per-output checks per step (e.g. `provision` produces `pod_handle.json` checked by `json_schema({"required":["pod_id","ssh","terminate_at"]})`). Keep the rich runner manifest as a debug artifact but don't make it the gate signal.

3. **Guaranteed teardown does not exist as an orchestrator primitive.** Reviewed `plan.py` (`on_exhaust: escalate|fail`), `dsl.py`, `gate.py`. There is no `finally` step, no `on_failure` hook, no `cleanup` lane. Three honest options, in order of preference:
   - **(a) Composite executor `external.runpod.session`** that takes a remote script + paths and internally does provision→exec→download→terminate with a Python `try/finally`. This is exactly what `run_pod_detached` already does. *Astrid's contract is satisfied because the executor either succeeds or fails — and in either case the pod is gone by the time it returns.* This is what vibecomfy needs today.
   - **(b) Separate `provision` / `exec` / `teardown` executors** for cases where you genuinely want exec/judge/refine cycles against the same pod (the `repeat.until(passes_judge)` pattern in `scene_render`). Plus a sweeper.
   - **(c) Both.** Ship the composite first; ship the split executors when an orchestrator actually needs to reuse a pod across steps.

4. **Pack layout — one folder, three (or four) executors.** The vibecomfy pack already declares two executors (`external.vibecomfy.run` + `external.vibecomfy.validate`) in a single `executor.yaml` (a JSON array under `executors`). That is the right precedent. Concretely:

   ```
   astrid/packs/external/runpod/
     __init__.py
     executor.yaml        # array of 3–4 executor defs
     run.py               # argparse dispatcher: provision|exec|teardown|session
     STAGE.md
     requirements.txt     # "runpod-lifecycle>=0.2"
   ```
   
   Executor ids: `external.runpod.provision`, `external.runpod.exec`, `external.runpod.teardown`, `external.runpod.session` (composite).

5. **`consumes`/`provides` sketch:**
   - `provision`: consumes nothing (or `runpod_config` JSON); provides `pod_handle` (`json_file` check).
   - `exec`: consumes `pod_handle` + `remote_script` (file or string input) + `local_root`; provides `exec_result` (`json_file` with `exit_code`, `artifact_dir`).
   - `teardown`: consumes `pod_handle`; provides `teardown_receipt`.
   - `session`: consumes `local_root` + `remote_script`; provides `exec_result` + `artifact_dir`. (Internally provisions + execs + tears down — no pod_handle escapes.)

6. **Detached/long-running.** Astrid executors are subprocesses; the parent (orchestrator runner) waits on them. A 4-hour training run is *fine* under this model — the executor just blocks. The Python-side `asyncio` and the polling loop in `run_pod_detached` are independent of Astrid's process model. There's no reason to invent an "async executor" — `session` just runs synchronously from Astrid's POV.

7. **The watchdog problem.** The `PodGuard` watchdog dies when the `provision` executor exits — confirmed. Fix:
   - The `pod_handle.json` written by `provision` includes `"terminate_at": "<ISO timestamp>"`.
   - Add `astrid.packs.external.runpod.sweep` (a command, not an executor) that lists all `pod_handle.json` under `runs/` and `astrid-projects/`, plus calls `discovery.list_pods` with the pack's `name_prefix`, and terminates anything past its `terminate_at` *or* without a corresponding live handle file. Wire it into `astrid doctor` (which already runs registry checks via `astrid/doctor.py`) and ship a `find-orphans` shortcut.
   - For the composite `session` executor, the watchdog *can* live in-process (it's the same model `run_pod` uses today and works because the executor blocks until done).
   - For `provision → exec → teardown` flows, the orchestrator's `repeat.until(...) max_iterations` + the sweep is the backstop.

8. **No existing remote/sandboxed execution concept in Astrid.** Grep for "runpod|remote.*exec|sandbox" in `astrid/` finds nothing except a passing reference in two docs files. This is the first one, which is good — no legacy shape to honour.

---

## Part D — synthesis

### Three-layer split

**`runpod-lifecycle` (v0.2.0) — generic shipping primitives**

```
src/runpod_lifecycle/
  api.py            (unchanged)
  config.py         (unchanged)
  discovery.py      (unchanged)
  events.py         (unchanged)
  lifecycle.py      (unchanged)
  pod.py            (+ Pod.upload_path, Pod.download_archive)
  ssh.py, storage.py (unchanged)
  guard.py          NEW — PodGuard, signal handlers
  shipping.py       NEW — UploadHeartbeat, _build_upload_tarball,
                          _iter_upload_files, _estimate_upload_payload,
                          _preflight_upload_disk, _upload_remote_script,
                          _download_artifacts
  runner.py         NEW — ship_and_run, ship_and_run_detached
  cli.py            (+ exec / ship / fetch / run subcommands)
```

**`vibecomfy` (post-shrink)**

```
scripts/runpod_runner.py  shrinks to ~250 lines:
  - imports run_pod / run_pod_detached from runpod_lifecycle.runner
  - keeps the vibecomfy-specific status_command builder
  - keeps the artifact-format readers (_parse_tsv, _collect_*,
    _build_artifact_manifest, _write_artifact_report, _print_detached_summary)
  - keeps _runpod_config_kwargs (env-var convention)
  - REMOTE_ROOT, DEFAULT_UPLOAD_EXCLUDES stay as module constants

scripts/runpod_validate.py, runpod_model_matrix.py,
scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py — unchanged in behavior; their imports of
DEFAULT_UPLOAD_EXCLUDES / REMOTE_ROOT / run_pod[_detached] keep working
because runpod_runner re-exports them.

vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py — unchanged.
```

**`Astrid/astrid/packs/external/runpod/`**

```
__init__.py
executor.yaml      # 4 executors
run.py             # subcommands: provision | exec | teardown | session
STAGE.md
requirements.txt   # runpod-lifecycle>=0.2
```

### New `runpod-lifecycle` public surface

```python
# In runpod_lifecycle (added to __all__)

class PodGuard:
    def __init__(self, *, name_prefix, max_runtime_seconds, hooks=None): ...
    async def launch(self, config) -> Pod: ...
    async def terminate(self) -> None: ...

class UploadProgress:  # was UploadHeartbeat
    def __init__(self, *, label, every_seconds=10.0, every_files=250): ...
    def tick(self, *, files=0, bytes_added=0, force=False): ...

async def ship_and_run(
    config: RunPodConfig,
    *, local_root: Path, remote_root: str,
    remote_script: str, excludes: Iterable[str] = (),
    upload_mode: Literal["sftp_walk", "tarball"] = "sftp_walk",
    timeout: int, name_prefix: str = "pod",
    progress: UploadProgress | None = None,
) -> RunResult: ...

async def ship_and_run_detached(
    config, *, local_root, remote_root, remote_script,
    excludes=(), upload_mode="sftp_walk",
    timeout, poll_interval=60, name_prefix="pod",
    artifact_paths: tuple[str, ...] = (),
    artifact_dir: Path | None = None,
    on_poll: Callable[[PollSnapshot], None] | None = None,
) -> DetachedRunResult: ...
```

`RunResult` carries `exit_code`, `pod_id`, `stdout`, `stderr`. `DetachedRunResult` adds `artifact_dir: Path | None`, `terminated: bool`. Neither produces a `manifest.json` — the caller (vibecomfy or Astrid) writes its own.

`Pod.upload_path(...)` and `Pod.download_archive(...)` wrap the lifted shipping helpers so callers that don't want the full `ship_and_run` can compose.

### Astrid pack shape

`executor.yaml` (sketched, one JSON file with 4 entries):

- **`external.runpod.provision`** — `inputs: [gpu_type?, storage_name?, max_runtime_seconds?, name_prefix?]`; `outputs: [pod_handle (json_file, default {out}/pod_handle.json)]`. `run.py provision` calls `lifecycle.launch(config)`, awaits `wait_ready`, writes `{pod_id, ssh, name, terminate_at, config_snapshot}` to the handle path. Does *not* terminate.

- **`external.runpod.exec`** — `inputs: [pod_handle (file, required), local_root (dir, optional), remote_root (string), remote_script (file or string), timeout, upload_mode, excludes]`; `outputs: [exec_result (json_file), artifact_dir (dir)]`. `run.py exec` reads the handle, calls `discovery.get_pod(pod_id, config)`, runs `ship_and_run_detached` with `terminate=False` (a new flag — neither current `run_pod` nor `run_pod_detached` keeps the pod alive after exit, that needs to be added to the lifted runner).

- **`external.runpod.teardown`** — `inputs: [pod_handle]`; `outputs: [teardown_receipt (json_file)]`. Calls `discovery.terminate(pod_id, api_key)`. Idempotent — handles "pod already terminated".

- **`external.runpod.session`** — composite. `inputs: [local_root, remote_root, remote_script, timeout, upload_mode, excludes, max_runtime_seconds, gpu_type?, storage_name?]`; `outputs: [exec_result, artifact_dir]`. Pure `ship_and_run_detached`. Guarantees teardown via Python `try/finally`. **This is the one vibecomfy and `lora_train` should use day-1.** The split provision/exec/teardown is for `scene_render`'s `repeat.until(passes_judge)` per-shot loop, *if* it needs to keep the pod warm across iterations (open question — see below).

`run.py` is a thin argparse dispatcher (same shape as `vibecomfy/run.py`).

### Migration order

1. **v0.2 of `runpod-lifecycle`** — lift `PodGuard`, `UploadHeartbeat`, shipping helpers, `ship_and_run`/`ship_and_run_detached`. Add `Pod.upload_path`/`download_archive`. Critically: `run_pod` in vibecomfy stays a working facade by re-importing from the lifecycle package. *No behavior change in vibecomfy at this stage.* Ship to PyPI/git, pin in vibecomfy.
2. **Shrink vibecomfy** — replace local `run_pod`/`run_pod_detached` with thin wrappers that call the lifecycle versions and post-process the downloaded archive into the manifest. `runpod_validate.py`/`runpod_model_matrix.py`/`runpod_corpus_matrix.py` keep importing `run_pod`/`run_pod_detached` and `REMOTE_ROOT`/`DEFAULT_UPLOAD_EXCLUDES` from the same module — no caller change.
3. **Build the Astrid pack** — `astrid/packs/external/runpod/` with `session` first, then `provision`/`exec`/`teardown`. Wire `lora_train` (Phase 2 in `project.md`) onto `session`.
4. **Add the sweep** — `astrid/packs/external/runpod/sweep.py` + integrate with `astrid doctor`.

Risks:
- Multi-repo coordination: `runpod-lifecycle` is consumed today via a sibling-checkout path (`VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_LIFECYCLE_ROOT` in `runpod_runner.py:50` and `vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py:14`). Astrid will need the same dance, or a real `pip install`. Recommend pinning by git tag (`v0.2.0`) once published.
- Backward compat: vibecomfy callers use `DEFAULT_UPLOAD_EXCLUDES` and `REMOTE_ROOT` from `runpod_runner`. Keep those as module constants in the post-shrink file.
- Versioning: bump `runpod-lifecycle` to `0.2.0` (breaking only by addition); vibecomfy pins explicitly. Astrid pins via its own `requirements.txt`.

---

## Open questions

1. **Does `scene_render`'s per-shot `repeat.until(passes_judge)` loop want a hot pod across iterations, or is each shot a fresh `session`?** If hot, you genuinely need `provision`/`exec`/`teardown` split + the sweeper. If cold (each shot reprovisions), `session` is enough and the split executors can wait. My read of `project.md` says hot — but only because per-shot reprovisioning would be wasteful, not because the doc specifies it.

2. **Where does `pod_handle.json` *live* across executor steps?** Astrid resolves output paths through `{out}` and `path_template`. Confirm with the user whether the convention is "the provision executor's `{out}/pod_handle.json` is read by `exec` via `--input pod_handle=...`" — that's what the runner code (`_placeholder_values`, `_request_values`) supports, but I want explicit confirmation that's the intended shape rather than e.g. a project-scoped artifact registry.

3. **`astrid doctor` integration of the sweeper.** Doctor today is read-only diagnostics (`run_checks` in `astrid/doctor.py`). Sweeping (= actually terminating pods) is mutation. Probably wants its own verb (`astrid runpod sweep` or `astrid orchestrators run external.runpod.sweep`), with doctor merely *reporting* stale handles. Confirm intent.

4. **Should `session` produce the vibecomfy-style `manifest.json` + `report.md` at all?** Today those are *vibecomfy's* artifact-collection format. For Astrid's `lora_train` and `scene_render`, the natural produces are checkpoint paths and clip files, not a `manifest.json` summarising TSV rows. I'd let `session` produce only `exec_result.json` (exit_code, artifact_dir, pod_id, terminated) and let each consuming pack post-process the artifact dir into its own gate-checked outputs. But that's a call for the user.

5. **The corpus_matrix scope.** `runpod_corpus_matrix.py` is 871 lines and tightly couples script generation to vibecomfy's registry. None of that is generic. Confirmed it stays in vibecomfy unchanged — but worth flagging that the *poll-loop status_command* it generates is fundamentally a "vibecomfy-shaped progress probe". If Astrid orchestrators want progress visibility into a detached run, they'll need their own equivalent — likely just `tail -n 100 some.log` is enough.
